# GUARD WORK-ORDERS — the live fix board (read after AUDIT-COVERAGE-LIVE.md, before any block)

## ★★ STANDING RULE — QBO 2023–2025 FIGURES ARE STILL MOVING. DO NOT FILE NUMERIC DELTAS AS DEFECTS.

**Owner-stated 2026-08-07.** The internal accountant (**Martin**) is **still adjusting FY2023, FY2024 and
FY2025** in QuickBooks as a consequence of the **embezzlement by Ignacio Muñoz**. **Those figures will
keep changing until the reconciliation is complete.**

**WHAT THIS MEANS FOR EVERY LANE — this is a "do not file" rule, and it is not optional:**

1. **A numeric difference between QBO and TMS for FY2023–FY2025 is EXPECTED STATE, not a defect.** TMS
   cloned a snapshot; QBO has moved since and will move again. Filing the delta as a discrepancy is the
   `expected-state-recorded-as-failure` anti-pattern, and at these magnitudes it would send a lane
   chasing a number that is *supposed* to change.
2. **NEVER "fix" TMS to match a QBO figure from those years.** The locked strategy is
   **clone-as-is-then-adjust**: clone faithfully, then book post-opening adjusting entries — or let
   Martin fix it at source. Reaching in to force a match destroys the audit trail on both sides.
3. **NEVER write back to QBO.** Unchanged and absolute (`QBO_JE_PUSH_ENABLED` / `QBO_ENTITY_PUSH_ENABLED`
   default OFF). A lane "helpfully" correcting QBO would be corrupting the book of record **during an
   embezzlement reconciliation** — the one book that may be examined by counsel, a court, or an insurer.
4. **What IS legitimately reportable:** a **structural** defect that is true regardless of the numbers —
   a sign convention applied inconsistently, an account mapped to the wrong control account, a posting
   that never happens at all, a subledger that disagrees with its own GL **within TMS**. Those do not
   depend on Martin's figures and stay in scope.
5. **Record the number, do not act on it.** When a delta is measured, log it as a **tie-out input for
   when the close is finalised** — e.g. the **exact −$5,670.00** difference between the TMS opening entry
   and QBO's current A/R (battery item 65). That is useful; calling it a bug is not.

**CONTEXT THAT EXPLAINS THE CHART OF ACCOUNTS:** the A/R sub-accounts **"Unauthorized Expenses Ignacio
Muñoz" ($350,451.38)** and **"Unauthorized Expenses Anarely Alcazar" ($73,253.48)** on QBO's 12/31/2024
balance sheet are the embezzlement receivables. Per the locked decision they are **receivables pursued in
bankruptcy court — NOT written off and NOT reclassed to expense.** **No lane may "clean up" or reclassify
them.** They are evidence.

**WHEN THIS RULE EXPIRES:** when the owner states the 2023–2025 reconciliation is complete. Until then it
governs, and the opening JE stays deferred by owner decision — **which is an owner-written deferral, the
one form the never-defer law explicitly allows.**

## ★ SHARED RESOURCE COORDINATION — DEBUGGED CHROME PORTS (owner-directed 2026-08-07)

**Why this exists:** the browser is a SHARED resource. Lanes were attaching to the same Chrome instance,
so one lane's navigation stole another lane's tab mid-flow, and a tab group was replaced under a running
verification. **Each lane gets its own remote-debugging port. Do not attach to a port that is not yours.**

| lane | role | **port** |
|---|---|---|
| **CC-1** | money / GL / WORM | **9222** |
| **CC-2** | GUARD — verify live, never build | **9223** |
| **CC-3** | mechanical / entity-scope / FE / CI-guards / live battery | **9224** |
| **CASCADE** | merger + scribe | **9225** |
| *(spare / ad-hoc)* | — | **9226+** |

**Launch YOUR port with its own profile** (a separate `--user-data-dir` is required — two Chrome
instances cannot share one profile, and omitting it silently attaches to the existing browser, which is
the collision this table exists to prevent):

```
/Applications/Google\ Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google\ Chrome \
  --remote-debugging-port=<YOUR PORT> \
  --user-data-dir="$HOME/.chrome-mcp-<YOUR LANE>" &
```

**RULES.**
1. **Never attach to another lane's port.** If your port is not answering, start YOUR instance — do not
   borrow one that is.
2. **Call `tabs_context_mcp` FIRST every session** and again after any unexpected navigation. Never reuse
   a tab id from a previous session; ids do not survive a restart.
3. **If the extension is disconnected, say so and switch to work that does not need it** (Neon reads,
   code reads, guards). **Do not silently retry** — a browser that is not answering is a fact to report,
   not a condition to loop on.
4. **Entity discipline still applies inside the browser**: confirm the company selector reads YOUR
   entity before any write. A shared browser can leave another lane's entity selected.

## ★★★ PERMANENT LAW — owner-locked 2026-08-07 (supreme; supersedes nothing, binds everything)

> **WE NEVER GUESS. WE NEVER DEFER. WE ALWAYS VERIFY. WE ALWAYS FIX, NOT PATCH. WE WANT PERMANENT FIXES
> ALWAYS.** — owner, verbatim, 2026-08-07

**ENFORCED, not aspirational.** Canonical text: `docs/law/NEVER-GUESS-NEVER-DEFER-ALWAYS-VERIFY-ALWAYS-FIX.md`.
Guard: `scripts/verify-no-patch-or-defer-language.mjs`, wired at
`scripts/verify-steps/2703-verify-no-patch-or-defer-language.mjs`, registered `enforced` in
`docs/law/LAW.json` as `LAW-2026-08-07-NEVER-GUESS-NEVER-DEFER-ALWAYS-VERIFY-ALWAYS-FIX`. **Per
LAW-2026-08-05-B2 ("LAW = ENFORCED GUARD, OR IT IS NOT LAW"), writing this rule as prose alone would
itself have been a patch.** Live proof: guard `--selftest` **17/17 PASS**, repo scan **exit 0** across
**1,164** active work orders; `verify-law-registry` **exit 0** (44 laws, 38 enforced, every guard file
resolves).

**1. NEVER GUESS.** A fact requires evidence produced THIS SESSION from a PRIMARY source — the running
app, a live DB row, a job log, the file itself. Memory, a doc, a skill, another agent's "verified", and
inference from a similar case are not evidence. A `0` is not a verdict without the completeness
discriminator on the SAME table. **A passing guard is not proof the law it encodes holds — read what the
guard actually gates.** Cannot verify? The output is **"UNVERIFIED — needs live check"**, never a guess.

**2. NEVER DEFER.** The root problem is fixed in this change. No `TODO fix later`, no "ship now, fix
isolation later", no "CI green is enough". The ONLY allowed deferral is an owner-written tracker entry
with a future block id. If part of a scope is genuinely blocked, finish every other part IN FULL and say
plainly what is left and why — **silence about a gap is deferral.**

**3. ALWAYS VERIFY.** Done = real schema + local green + CI green + merged + **deploy verified live** +
behaviour confirmed on prod. **CI-green is the floor. Merged is not done. Deployed is not done until the
health SHA matches.** Strongest evidence, ascending: read it → create the row yourself and read it back →
**controlled before/after with a baseline captured first**. **Check before you file** — a finding that
dissolves on one more query was never a finding.

**4. ALWAYS FIX, NEVER PATCH.** Complete only when ALL hold: (a) root cause corrected **at its source**;
(b) a guard that **fails on the bug and passes on the fix**, wired via `scripts/verify-steps/NNNN-*.mjs`;
(c) **live proof on prod**; (d) **the class addressed** — same shape elsewhere is fixed or explicitly
bounded with evidence. **Forbidden as "fixes":** silencing a guard, widening a constraint to turn red
green, special-casing the row that failed, editing the message instead of the behaviour, reverting a
correct change to dodge a check, or "fixing" a symptom whose root cause you have not located.
**Prefer the permanent mechanism: a DB constraint beats a guard; a guard beats a convention; a convention
beats a comment.** If a future writer can silently recreate the defect, the fix is not permanent.
**When a correct fix turns something red, that redness IS the fix working — finish the work it points at,
do not silence it.**

## ★★ PERMANENT LAW — owner-locked 2026-08-05 (supreme; applies to every agent, every session)

**1. FINDINGS FLOW AGENT → BOARD → AGENT, NEVER THROUGH THE OWNER.** Find a defect in another lane →
**WRITE an OPEN row into `docs/audit/GUARD-WORKORDERS.md` yourself and commit it**; the target coder pulls
it on their next loop. **The owner is NOT a message bus — ever, in any session.**

**2. LAW = ENFORCED GUARD, OR IT IS NOT LAW** (phased). Every NEW rule ships with a guard registered in
`docs/law/LAW.json`. `verify-law-registry.mjs` is a required check (<2s, existence-only) and fails the
build if a registered law's guard file is missing. Old rules migrate as a backlog class. Judgment rules
stay judgment.

**3. ROLES.** CC-1 = money / GL / WORM. CC-3 = mechanical / entity-scope / FE / CI-guards. CC-2 = GUARD,
**verify live, never build**. CASCADE = merger (direct merge API — auto-merge is broken on our rulesets,
community #190610).

**4. ENTITY + DATA LAW.** VOID = reversal; **nothing is deletable**. TRANSP / USMCA own **no assets
today**. **ALL TMS-native data is TEST** — only the TRANSP QBO mirror is real. **RLS is NOT a backstop for
Owner sessions**: `org.user_accessible_company_ids()` returns EVERY active company when the role is Owner,
so **every unscoped read is load-bearing on its own predicate**.

**5. EVERY LOOP.** read board → **grep-verify the card against main** → build **ONE complete atomic block**
→ found another lane's defect? **write it to the board** → push → next. Never idle, never pause to
summarize, never half-edit.


**Maintained by GUARD. Purpose: no coder idles.** `AUDIT-COVERAGE-LIVE.md` says WHAT is broken (finding +
live evidence). This board says WHO owns it, the FIX REQUIREMENT + the standard it must meet, and the GUARD
status. Pull the top `OPEN` item in your lane and start — do not wait to be told, do not re-audit a row that
already has an owner here.

## ⚑ TOP PRIORITY — LIVE TRANSACTION BATTERY on USMCA (owner-directed 2026-08-06, NON-STOP)

**Owner (Jorge) directed: create EVERY transaction type end-to-end, live, on the USMCA test entity, non-stop.**
This is the end-to-end test-load run that lights up the empty subledgers and drains the stuck classes
(CLS-MONEY-HOLD, CLS-DISP-WIRE-04/05/06/07/08/10, CLS-BANK-MATCH, CLS-LINKAGE-ONEWAY). ALL USMCA data is TEST
data (PERMANENT LAW §2) — creating it is safe and is the point. **USMCA ONLY — never TRANSP (real QBO books).**

Run each through the ACTUAL app UI (@browser, app.ih35dispatch.com, USMCA entity selected) — NOT direct SQL —
because the create-path validators are exactly where defects hide (bill category-line validator, WO cost-line
validator, inline +Create native-GET, etc.). For EACH: create it, confirm it persists on reload, confirm the
expected GL/subledger row appears on Neon prod (balanced JE where posting applies), record PASS/FAIL + the id.

BATTERY (do in order; loop back to the top and vary amounts/vendors when the list is done):
  Master data:  create vendor · edit vendor · change vendor type · create customer · edit customer ·
                create item/expense-category · create driver · create unit · create trailer
  AP:           create bill (each category: fuel/repair/maintenance/misc) · bill payment · vendor credit /
                credit memo · purchase order · recurring bill
  AR:           create invoice (manual + from-load) · send invoice · customer payment · credit memo (AR)
  Expenses:     create expense (each payment account) · expense with load link
  Maintenance:  work order · repair order · maintenance order · add parts/labor lines · close WO → bill
  Dispatch:     book load · assign driver+unit · dispatch · capture departure (actual_departure_at!) ·
                POD capture · BOL generate · deliver · cancel load · rebook a cancelled load
  Settlement:   run a driver settlement (lights driver_settlements/deductions/escrow)
  Banking:      categorize a bank transaction · match a bank tx to a bill/invoice
  Factoring:    create a factoring advance (USMCA)

For each result write a row to docs/audit/LIVE-TXN-BATTERY-2026-08-06.md (id, type, persisted?, GL row?, PASS/FAIL).
A FAIL is a real defect → write it as an OPEN board row for the owning builder lane. NEVER route through the owner.
Status: **OPEN — TOP PRIORITY, NON-STOP**, owner-directed. This card never "completes" — loop it.

## How to use this board

1. Read `docs/audit/AUDIT-COVERAGE-LIVE.md` (the findings) + this file (the assignments + requirements).
2. Take the highest `OPEN` item in **your lane** below.
3. Build to the **Fix requirement** + the cited standard. Author the compliant block (18 git-gate keys, DoD/
   VERIFY, guard with `--selftest`, linkage declaration) — CI enforces it.
4. **Apply on Neon yourself** (you have access; the owner does not hand-run Neon) after the gates pass.
5. Set the audit row `Status = FIXED (PR #nnnn)`. Post the merge sha here + to GUARD.
6. **GUARD verifies** in git (file+line on main, guard wired, deploy sha) + on Neon (posting balanced, linkage
   both-ways, positive control) and flips the row to `VERIFIED`. A coder `FIXED` is a claim until then.

## Lanes

- **CLAUDE CODER** — financial cluster: `accounting.*`/`catalogs.accounts`, migrations, posting/GL, role
  designations, the USMCA chart, the seed battery.
- **CURSOR** — frontend/UI, measurability, docs, non-financial backend, catalog seeds, KPI/parity fixes.
- **CASCADE** — audit writer: finish the Layer-C sweep, re-test the UNVERIFIED D/E rows, design-bar pass.

## ★★★ CC-3 DISPATCH — 2026-08-07 · COMPLETE + PERMANENT FIXES ONLY (no patches)

**OWNER RULE, BINDING ON EVERY ROW BELOW (CLAUDE.md §10):** *we never patch — we make complete and
permanent fixes.* A row is DONE only when **(1)** the root cause is fixed at its source, **(2)** a guard
exists that fails on the bug and passes on the fix, and **(3)** live proof is produced on prod. Green CI
is the floor, not the verdict. **Each row below also names THE PATCH THAT MUST NOT BE APPLIED** — for
most of these the tempting shallow fix makes CI green while leaving the defect, or is actively worse.

**★ ROUTING CORRECTION — READ FIRST.** Rows found by this lane were previously tagged `CC-2 / mechanical`.
**That is wrong per this board's own supreme law §3**, which says **CC-1 = money/GL/WORM · CC-3 =
mechanical/entity-scope/FE/CI-guards · CC-2 = GUARD, verify live, NEVER BUILD.** Routing build work to
CC-2 guarantees it is never built. **Mechanical/FE rows below are therefore addressed to the
MECHANICAL/FE lane (CC-3 role per §3), not CC-2.** This lane's current instance is the live verifier and
does not fix what it verified (maker != checker) — it owns CI-guards only.

### ORDER OF WORK — by blast radius, not by discovery order

**TIER 1 — MOVES CASH OR MISSTATES THE LEDGER (CC-1, money):**

1. **`LV-PAYABLE-SELECTOR-OFFERS-VOIDED-BILLS`** — voided bills are offered for payment in TWO
   independent pickers. **COMPLETE FIX:** every payable/open-bill query filters on the authoritative
   void column, and the status/void pairing is enforced **in the database** (CHECK or trigger) so no
   future writer can desync them. **DO NOT PATCH BY:** fixing only the one selector you found, or by
   back-filling `status` on today's rows — both leave the next writer free to recreate it.
   **PERMANENT because:** a DB constraint cannot be forgotten by a new code path.

2. **`LV-BILLPAY-VOID-NO-REVERSAL`** — void posts no reversing entry though the UI promises one.
   **COMPLETE FIX:** post the equal-and-opposite entry **through the existing poster** and write
   `reverses_je_id` / `reversed_by_je_id` both ways. **THE WORKING REFERENCE IS ALREADY IN-REPO** — the
   JE-void path does all of this correctly; copy it. **DO NOT PATCH BY:** writing new GL math, or by
   softening the UI text to stop promising a reversal — the promise is correct, the code is wrong.

3. **`LV-PAY-SETTLE-NOPOST`** — customer payments never post; A/R subledger vs GL control out by
   **$1,287.25** and growing. **COMPLETE FIX:** wire payment-received to the existing poster
   (DR selected deposit account / CR A/R), honouring the `Deposited to` selection, and reverse on void.
   **A/P is the working mirror — copy it.** **DO NOT PATCH BY:** a per-row "payments must post" check
   alone — **the guard must be the subledger-to-GL tie-out per control account**, because only the
   tie-out catches an invoice that reads `paid` against a GL still carrying it in full.

4. **`LV-REVREC-NOT-FIRING`** — cross-connection read-your-own-writes: `loads.routes.ts:1295` stamps
   `actual_departure_at` on connection A (uncommitted), `:1335` reads it on connection B via
   `withLuciaBypass`. **COMPLETE FIX:** fire the latch **after the transaction commits**.
   **DO NOT PATCH BY (this one is dangerous):** loosening the `missing_delivery_evidence` gate so the
   latch posts on status alone. That gate is CORRECT ASC-606 conservatism — loosening it starts
   recognising **unearned revenue**, which is worse than the bug. Also **do not** pass the route's
   `client` into the poster: it needs the `withLuciaBypass` RLS context.

5. **`LV-VOID-INVARIANT-BOTH-WAYS` + `LV-CANCEL-VOIDS-STATUS-ONLY` + `LV-AR/AP-OPEN-INCLUDES-VOIDED`** —
   **these are ONE root cause with four symptoms: void state is not authoritative anywhere in A/P.**
   **COMPLETE FIX:** one piece of work — enforce `status='void' <-> voided_at IS NOT NULL` in the DB,
   correct the cancel path to stamp both, and make every open-balance aggregate exclude voided rows.
   **DO NOT PATCH BY:** fixing the four cards separately, or by a one-directional guard — the pairing
   is broken in BOTH directions (invoices: status set / timestamp missing; bills: timestamp set /
   status missing), so a one-sided assertion passes on half the corrupt rows.
   **TRAP:** `accounting.bill_payments` uses **`revoked_at`**, not `voided_at`; `vendor_credit_applications`
   uses `voided_reason`, invoices use `void_reason`. **A copy-paste fix WILL silently miss tables — you
   need a per-table map.**

6. **`LV-BANK-TWO-SIGN-CONVENTIONS`** — one column, two conventions (Relay Fuel Wallet positive, all
   else negative). **COMPLETE FIX:** normalise on write so `amount_cents` is a true magnitude with
   `is_credit` the sole direction carrier — which is the law two files already declare — or record the
   convention per account in the schema. **DO NOT PATCH BY:** special-casing Relay in each reader; that
   multiplies the trap. **VERIFY FIRST, DO NOT ASSUME:** whether the live recon engine actually
   mis-matches is UNVERIFIED — its comment is false for 96% of credits, but both sides may normalise
   identically and cancel. Trace the QBO side before changing behaviour.

7. **`LV-BANK-BANNER-SAYS-FLAG-OFF`** — the banking page tells operators categorize does not post; the
   flag is **ON for all three entities** and it does. **COMPLETE FIX:** render the **live per-entity
   override**, never the global default. **DO NOT PATCH BY:** editing the sentence to say "ON" — static
   copy asserting a flag state will rot again the moment the flag flips.

**TIER 2 — MECHANICAL / FE (mechanical lane per §3 — NOT CC-2):**

8. **`LV-LOAD-DETAIL-SHOWS-UNASSIGNED`** — `GET /dispatch/loads/:id` selects `l.*` from an 18-column
   view lacking `trip_type` / `assigned_unit_number` / `assigned_primary_driver_name`. **COMPLETE FIX:**
   mirror for the PRIMARY driver exactly what the same query already does for the SECONDARY, and add
   `trip_type`. **DO NOT PATCH BY:** having the UI fall back to a UUID — that is the defect this repo
   already fixed once elsewhere (`CLS-UUID-LABEL`).

9. **`LV-STOP-ZIP-DROPPED`** — `postal_code` is NULL on **0 of 18** stops while city/state persist.
   **COMPLETE FIX:** persist it on the booking path. **WHY IT IS NOT COSMETIC:** it is the PC*MILER
   routing key — without it, per-mile driver pay and IFTA jurisdiction miles are unreachable.

10. **`LV-DISPATCH-TOAST-LIES`** — "Load booked and dispatched" after an audited DOT override, while the
    load never left `assigned_not_dispatched`. **COMPLETE FIX:** report the status the SERVER returned.
    **An override audit trail attesting to an action that did not happen is worse than no override.**

11. **`LV-BULK-DELIVER-NOLATCH`** — bulk "Mark delivered" stamps delivery evidence and never latches.
    **COMPLETE FIX:** call the latch — **using item 40's after-commit ordering**, or you reproduce the
    Tier-1 #4 timing bug in a second place. **Note:** the widened guard on this branch is *correctly RED*
    on that file and cannot go green until this is done. That redness is the forcing function, not a
    problem to silence.

12. **`LV-EXPENSE-VOID-UNREACHABLE`** — the void endpoint exists, no UI reaches it, and the FE renders a
    `void` badge it can never produce. **COMPLETE FIX:** either surface the endpoint on the expense
    detail page, **or** state the read-only decision explicitly and remove the dead badge branch.
    **Either answer is fine; the current ambiguous state is the one that is not.**

13. **`SKILL-DRIFT-USMCA-HIDDEN`** — two AUTO-LOADED skills say USMCA is hidden/future with 0 balances;
    prod and this lane's live transactions disprove it. **COMPLETE FIX:** correct the skill text AND add
    a presence-ratchet so no auto-loaded skill can describe USMCA as hidden again. **DO NOT PATCH BY:**
    editing the prose only — an unguarded doc fact drifts back.

**TIER 3 — OWNED BY WHOEVER OWNS THE BLOCK:**

14. **`BLOCK-0280-02-BOILERPLATE-ACCEPTANCE`** — the abolished-hold half is **DONE** (removed at the root
    2026-08-07). What remains is typed machine-checkable `acceptance[]`. **CC-3 will not invent criteria
    for a revenue-GL block it does not own — that is guessing.**

**STANDING NOTE FOR EVERY ROW:** the live evidence, exact file:line, and the disproof conditions are in
`docs/audit/LIVE-TXN-BATTERY-2026-08-06.md`. Where this lane could not prove something it says
**UNVERIFIED** rather than guessing — those are marked in-row and must be verified, not assumed, before
code changes.

| **ACCT-F148 — `accounting.invoice_lines` cannot represent a credit, discount or write-down** | — | C | CC-1 money | `CHECK (line_total_cents >= 0)` means the table has NO representation for a negative line. This is not hypothetical: **155 real QBO lines** cannot be stored as-is — 74 `DiscountLineDetail` (all positive in QBO, meant to be subtracted, $1.75–$458.70) plus **81 `SalesItemLineDetail` carrying NEGATIVE amounts down to −$1,000**, which are credits/write-downs QBO booked as negative revenue. ACCT-F146 works around it by storing both as `line_type='adjustment'` at `abs()` and subtracting them in the tie-out — arithmetically identical to QBO (`TotalAmt = SUM(all SalesItem incl. negatives) − SUM(discounts)`) and semantically defensible, since a negative revenue line IS a credit. **But the workaround has a cost**: every future reader summing `line_total_cents` must know adjustments are subtractive, and a credit memo has no first-class representation. Proper fix is a schema decision (allow negative, or an explicit `sign`/`is_credit` column) — its own block, not something to bury in a backfill. | — | **OPEN** |

Real target 16,670 + 74 = **16,744**. Tie-out proven against QBO's own `TotalAmt`: `Σ SalesItem − Σ Discount == TotalAmt` on **12,063/12,063**, versus 11,989/12,063 without the discount term. | CI-F03a — CodeQL: the 2 `scripts/` findings FIXED (ReDoS + unnecessary `cat`) | — | C | CC-2 mechanical | `verify-no-swallow-on-money-paths.mjs:31` (js/redos) put `\s*` BOTH inside the alternation and around it while the group was starred, so a run of whitespace could split between them in exponentially many ways: on `"catch(){" + "//".repeat(1000)` the OLD regex **did not terminate within 5 minutes** (reproduced twice). Hoisted `\s` into the alternation as a single-char branch — every branch now consumes >=1 char and branches are distinguishable on their first/second char. **Semantics proven identical across all 8,866 .ts/.tsx/.mjs/.js files under apps/ + scripts/: 5 matches before, 5 after, 0 files differing**, and the guard still exits 1 on both an empty catch and a comment-only catch on a money path. `verify-schema-parity-window-bounded.mjs:58` (js/unnecessary-use-of-cat) spawned `cat` to read a local file -> `readFileSync`, which also cannot fail silently to an undefined `.stdout` when the binary is absent. | (this branch) | **FIXED — PR pending** |
| CI-F03b — CodeQL `js/user-controlled-bypass` on the OAuth CSRF check: FIXED (suppression mechanism built) | — | C | CC-2 mechanical | **Not deferred — the fix was mine.** The finding is a false positive: `parsedState.state !== storedState` in the Google OAuth callback is the RFC 6749 §10.12 CSRF defence. The attacker controls `parsedState.state`; they do NOT control `storedState` (HttpOnly cookie we set), so the comparison passes only when both sides came from this browser's own authorization request — removing it is what would create a vulnerability. **Root cause of the red was not the auth code but the gate**: `ci-codeql-enforce-sarif.mjs` counted every `run.results` entry and ignored SARIF's standard `result.suppressions`, so the repo had NO supported way to mark a false positive — the only way to clear one was to edit the flagged code, which on a security control is a dangerous incentive. FIXED both halves: the enforcer now honours accepted **inSource** suppressions and **PRINTS** them (`[suppressed:error] … justification: …`) so a suppression is auditable in the log, never a silent hole; **rejected** and **external** suppressions still fail the build. Auth gets a `// codeql[js/user-controlled-bypass]` comment with the reasoning inline. No runtime behaviour changed. | (this branch) | **FIXED — PR pending** |

The second was the literal text `set_config('app.operating_company_id', …)` inside the explanatory comment I had written above the real call. `GUC_SET_RE` runs on raw source with no comment handling. Two consequences, opposite in sign: a comment that MENTIONS the GUC causes a false FAILURE (what bit me), and — worse — a real `set_config` could be neutralised in the count by an unrelated comment, or an exemption argued in prose could read as code. | CLS-GUARD-READS-COMMENTS — FIXED at the root: offset-preserving comment masking | — | C | CC-2 mechanical | Four guards matched patterns against RAW source and so read prose as code: `verify-company-membership-assert` (counted a `set_config` written in a comment as a 2nd tenant-GUC site), `verify-sql-read-targets` (reported `au.uuid` does-not-exist AFTER the join was fixed, matching the pre-rename name in the comment), `verify-no-uuid-label-rendering` (flagged a NEW truncated-uuid label from the comment DOCUMENTING its removal), and my own `verify-scoped-company-context-asserts-first` (passed with its assert deleted, matching its own JSDoc). Mutation-proven both directions on both guards: a real violation in CODE still fails (exit 1); the identical text in a COMMENT no longer does (exit 0). | (this branch) | **FIXED — PR pending** |

## §2-D NOTE (do not let this class come back)

The Layer-D test is the **inline +Create inside a picker/wizard** — it must be **exercised** (click, fill, save,
confirm the row persists to the canonical table and appears selected, with NO native GET). It is NOT satisfied by:

- "the code is wired to POST" — the Book Load customer create *was* wired to `createCustomer()`, but a nested
  `<form>` HTML5-drop meant the POST never fired. Code inspection is the exact false signal.
- a `created_at` row from a **module-level** create modal — that proves the module create works, not the
  inline-picker create. They are different code paths (the module modal is not inside a wizard `<form>`).

Until an inline +Create is actually exercised and confirmed to persist, its row is `UNVERIFIED`, never `PASS`.

## The gate

Only **GUARD** writes `VERIFIED`, after independent live re-check (git for code, Neon for data, the app for UI).
Governed by `docs/audit/AUDIT-LAW-PERMANENT.md` (order C→B→A→D→E; a screenshot is not a Layer-E pass; "complete"
= 30/30 certified only).
| **CLOSED — already fixed by CC-1** `CI-F01` — CodeQL failing on every PR since 23:26 CT | — | C | **CC-1 (done)** | CC-2 measured this while triaging its own PRs and filed it before checking whether another lane had it. **CC-1 had already diagnosed it more precisely and shipped the fix**, so this row is corrected rather than left to send someone re-investigating. **CC-2's reading:** CodeQL completes analysis then fails at upload with `Code scanning is not enabled for this repository`; a dated regression — the workflow on main was success at 15:13 and 15:32 CT and failure at 23:26. Directionally right, but it stopped at the symptom. **CC-1's actual root cause (`scripts/ci-codeql-enforce-sarif.mjs`, now on main):** publishing SARIF is a **GitHub Advanced Security** feature, free on public repos and licensed on private ones — **the repository went PRIVATE on 2026-08-06**, so the upload began returning 403 while the scan itself stayed healthy (4,639 TS + 4,242 JS files analysed, zero findings). **Their fix is the right shape and worth copying:** they did NOT reach for `continue-on-error`, which would have produced a green badge verifying nothing — the fake-green this repo's rules forbid. The analysis still runs in full and the new script reads the SARIF from disk, failing the build on real error-severity findings. **Standing note for this board:** the parse errors that appear above the failure in those logs are pre-existing deliberately-malformed test fixtures under `scripts/__tests__/fixtures/master-data-write-routes/**` — noise, not the failure. **Lesson CC-2 is recording against itself:** grep the board and `git log origin/main` for an owner before filing a cross-lane finding; this one cost a branch. | — | **CLOSED — superseded by CC-1's fix on main 2026-08-06** |
| **CORRECTED — NOT A DEFECT** `LV-TXN-005` (Kanban half): the "Loaded" lane does **not** send a rejected status | — | C | **CC-2 (self-correction)** | CC-3 filed LV-TXN-005 claiming the board renders a "Loaded" lane whose drop the API rejects with HTTP 400. **That claim was WRONG and is withdrawn.** The measured half was right — `PATCH /api/v1/mdata/loads/:id/status` does reject `new_status:'loaded'` — but the Loaded lane never sends it: `DispatchKanban.tsx` defines it as `{ key: "loaded", title: "Loaded", statuses: [], dropStatus: "in_transit" }`, i.e. a VISUAL STAGING column that holds no load status and whose drop is `in_transit`, which is accepted. The defect was inferred from one true fact plus an assumption about the other end. **FULL CROSS-CHECK, all 11 lanes vs `loadStatusSchema` (20 accepted values):** Awaiting assignment→planned, Booked unassigned→booked, Assigned→assigned, Dispatched→dispatched, At pickup→at_pickup, **Loaded→in_transit**, In transit→in_transit, At delivery→at_delivery, **Delivered→delivered_pending_docs**, Completed→closed, Cancelled→cancelled — **every one accepted.** The Delivered lane already carries an in-code comment explaining it uses `delivered_pending_docs` deliberately so the mdata path stamps `actual_departure_at` (WIRE-07), so the vocabulary difference between the two endpoints is HANDLED, not a defect either. **NO BUILDER TIME SHOULD BE SPENT ON THE KANBAN HALF OF LV-TXN-005.** **What was actually missing — and is now fixed:** nothing asserted the two ends agree. `scripts/verify-kanban-dropstatus-contract.mjs` cross-references `KANBAN_STATUS_GROUPS[].dropStatus` against `loadStatusSchema`, registered as `LAW-2026-08-06-KANBAN-DROPSTATUS-CONTRACT`. Mutation-proven 5/5 on synthetic cases AND against the REAL file by planting the exact defect CC-3 believed existed (`dropStatus: "loaded"`) → exit 1 naming the lane; restored → exit 0. It also refuses to pass vacuously if zero lanes or an unreadable enum are found. **The lesson generalises:** the two ends live in separate files owned by separate lanes, so they drift independently and nothing cheap connected them — which is exactly why an incorrect inference about the pair survived long enough to be filed. | — | **CLOSED — CC-2-verified 2026-08-06; guard added so the real version cannot ship** |
| **OPEN** `CLS-SILENT-CAP` — the class **cannot be drained by CC-2 alone**, and one instance chains into `CLS-UUID-LABEL` | — | C | **CC-2 (5 done)** + **CC-1 (3 money-path)** | CC-2 drained 7 of 61 offenders on `fix/cls-silent-cap-truncation-disclosure` (baseline **61 → 54**) using a new shared helper `apps/frontend/src/lib/list-cap.ts` (`listCapInfo` / `capNotice`, 10 unit tests). **TWO STRUCTURAL FINDINGS THAT CHANGE HOW THIS CLASS MUST BE FINISHED.** **(1) THE CLASS SPANS BOTH LANES — CC-2 CANNOT DRAIN IT.** Of the 7 files confirmed truncating on prod TODAY (matched by the ACTUAL `list(Vendors|Customers)(… limit: N)` call shape, not a filename guess), **3 are money-path**: `pages/accounting/BillsPage.tsx`, `pages/accounting/VendorCreditsPage.tsx`, `pages/accounting/bills/RecurringBillCreate.tsx` (all cap 1000, each hiding 1,836 of 2,836 vendors). A commit touching `pages/(accounting|banking)` is classified money and requires a `MODULE_PROGRESS: accounting|banking N of M` assertion against CC-1's scoreboard — not CC-2's to assert. `pages/banking/RecordCCPaymentModal.tsx` was fixed, verified, then **deliberately reverted out** for this reason and is back in the baseline (which is why the count is 54, not 53). **CC-1 must take those 4 money-path pickers or the class stays open regardless of CC-2's work.** **(2) THE CAP DOES NOT ONLY HIDE ROWS — IT CORRUPTS LABELS ON ROWS THAT ARE SHOWN.** `pages/inventory/InventoryPartsStockPage.tsx` builds `vendorNameById` from the capped 1,000-row fetch (line 145), resolves `vendor_label: vendorNameById.get(vendorId) ?? null` (line 122), and the column renders `label={row.vendor_label ?? row.vendor_id.slice(0, 8)}` (line 68). **So a part whose vendor falls outside the first 1,000 of 2,836 renders its vendor as a RAW 8-CHARACTER UUID FRAGMENT instead of a name** — `CLS-SILENT-CAP` producing a `CLS-UUID-LABEL` symptom. Neither guard catches the chain: the silent-cap guard sees boundary words in the file and clears it, and the uuid-label guard sees a `?? fallback` rather than a bare uuid render. **Whoever drains either class must fix this file for BOTH, and the fix is to resolve the label server-side (or fetch by the ids actually on the page), not to raise the cap.** **STANDING CAUTION FOR ANY DRAINER:** `verify-no-silent-list-caps.mjs`'s `HANDLES_BOUNDARY` clears a file if the bare word `total` or `count` appears ANYWHERE in it, so this baseline **can be drained cosmetically without fixing anything**. All 7 CC-2 fixes are real (named cap constant read by both the fetch and the check, EXACT truncation from the server `total`, notice rendered at the correct picker — verified on the two files that have two adjacent pickers). Do not trust a future baseline drop without reading the diff. | — | **OPEN — CC-2 partial 2026-08-06; 4 money-path files awaiting CC-1** |
| **OPEN** `LV-TXN-002` — the load detail drawer reports a driver-assigned load as **"Unassigned"** | — | C | **CC-2 / mechanical+routes+FE** | Found by CC-3 while live-draining `CLS-MONEY-HOLD` on USMCA. **`GET /api/v1/dispatch/loads/:id` is STRUCTURALLY INCAPABLE of returning the primary driver name or the unit number.** Live on deployed SHA `e6343f4`, load `L-20260802-0258` (`a6f8a7ec-…`, USMCA): the drawer renders **DRIVER: `Unassigned`** and **TRUCK UNIT: `—`**, while the dispatch LIST row for the SAME load on the SAME screen shows **"Juan USMCA-Battery" / "USMCA-001"**. **THE DATA IS CORRECT — this is not an entity leak and not bad data:** Neon prod confirms `assigned_primary_driver_id = 88c04cf5-…` → "Juan USMCA-Battery" with `operating_company_id = 5c854333` = **the load's own opco**, and `assigned_unit_id = bb1e77ab-…` → `USMCA-001`, `owner_company_id = b49a737b` (**TRK**) / `currently_leased_to_company_id = 5c854333` (**USMCA**) — the ordinary TRK-owns/USMCA-leases case. **ROOT CAUSE (verified against PROD `information_schema`, not inferred):** the route selects `l.*` from `views.dispatch_load_with_driver_status`, and that view exposes **only** `assigned_primary_driver_id` / `assigned_secondary_driver_id` / `assigned_unit_id` — it has **no `assigned_primary_driver_name` and no `assigned_unit_number` column at all**. The route then joins `mdata.drivers sd` to resolve **`assigned_secondary_driver_name`** and never joins for the PRIMARY driver or for `mdata.units`. Frontend `LoadDetailDrawer.tsx:372,374` reads `load.assigned_unit_number ?? "—"` and `load.assigned_primary_driver_name ?? "Unassigned"`, so `undefined` renders as "Unassigned". **That the SECONDARY (team) driver name IS resolved while the PRIMARY is not is the evidence this is oversight, not design.** **SCOPE — WIDER THAN THE TEST ENTITY:** no entity predicate is involved, so this affects **every load in every entity, always**; it is not a USMCA artifact. **OPERATIONAL CONSEQUENCE (why major, not cosmetic):** this is the screen a dispatcher opens to work a load; it states the load has no driver and no truck when it has both, inviting double-assignment or an "uncovered load" escalation on a covered load. **FIX REQUIREMENT:** copy the ALREADY-CORRECT sibling query at `apps/backend/src/mdata/loads.routes.ts:636` — `LEFT JOIN mdata.units u ON u.id = l.assigned_unit_id AND COALESCE(u.currently_leased_to_company_id, u.owner_company_id) = l.operating_company_id` and `LEFT JOIN mdata.drivers d ON d.id = l.assigned_primary_driver_id AND d.operating_company_id = l.operating_company_id`. Do NOT add the columns to the view unscoped. **PAYLOAD-LEVEL PROOF (live, `credentials:'include'`, deployed `e6343f4`):** the API response object has `'assigned_primary_driver_name' in payload` = **false** and `'assigned_unit_number' in payload` = **false** (fields ABSENT, not null), while `'assigned_secondary_driver_name' in payload` = **true** and both `assigned_primary_driver_id` (`88c04cf5-…`) and `assigned_unit_id` (`bb1e77ab-…`) are **non-null**. Measured at the contract boundary — no UI/DOM/screenshot. **GUARD:** assert the `/dispatch/loads/:id` payload carries `assigned_primary_driver_name` + `assigned_unit_number` whenever the corresponding `*_id` is non-null — a guard over the SQL predicate alone reproduces this exact miss, because the predicate is not what is wrong. Mutation-prove it. | — | **OPEN (major) — CC-3-measured live 2026-08-06, awaiting CC-2 build** |
| **OPEN (P0)** `LV-TXN-004` — ★ the office status control calls the endpoint that SKIPS revenue recognition AND settlement creation | — | C | **CC-1 (money)** · mechanical half CC-2 | **THE ROOT OF `CLS-MONEY-HOLD`. Found by RUNNING the business event on USMCA, not by reading code.** CC-3 walked load `L-20260802-0258` (`a6f8a7ec-…`) `assigned_not_dispatched → dispatched → at_pickup → in_transit → at_delivery → delivered`, every step HTTP 200, using the endpoint the app's own Kanban calls. **Result on Neon prod (bypass in-statement, positive control `accounting.bills`=16,255 in the SAME statement): `mdata.loads.status='delivered'` ✓, but `driver_finance.driver_settlements`=0 with `n_tup_ins` still 11 — UNCHANGED from the pre-walk baseline, so the walk created ZERO settlements; `accounting.posting_batches` where `source_transaction_id`=this load = 0 → NO revenue recognition; `%settle%` audit events in the last 20 min = 0.** **ROOT CAUSE — two status endpoints, only one carries the money side-effects:** `PATCH /api/v1/dispatch/loads/:id/transition` (`dispatch/loads.routes.ts:1215`) calls **`postLoadRevenueLatch`** (`:1335`) **and `pingSettlementOnLoadEvent`** (`:1348`); `PATCH /api/v1/mdata/loads/:id/status` (`mdata/loads.routes.ts:838`) calls **ONLY `latchOnDeliveryEvidence`** — it neither imports nor calls the other two (verified against the file's import list). The frontend's ONLY office-side status control is wired to the second: `Dispatch.tsx:439 onStatusDrop` → `api/loads.ts:263 updateLoadStatus` → `/mdata/loads/:id/status`. **So every load a dispatcher advances on the board silently skips revrec and never opens a driver settlement.** **This explains the whole class in one call:** `pre-settlements/open-by-driver` (`pre-settlement.routes.ts:51`) selects `FROM driver_finance.driver_settlements WHERE settlement_model='load_bookended' AND trip_closed_at IS NULL` — it lists ALREADY-OPEN settlements and does NOT derive candidates from delivered loads; the only writer of that row is `pingSettlementOnLoadEvent`. No ping → no settlement row → pre-settlements `[]` → no pay run → no lines → no GL. **HOLD-001 is ONE MISSING CALL, not three empty tables.** **TWO FALSE LEADS RULED OUT, so nobody re-chases them:** (1) `settlement_lines`=0 right after delivery is EXPECTED — those rows carry a `settlement_id` and are written by the settlement engine at pay-run time (`settlement-engine.ts:147+`), never at delivery; (2) the driver is NOT unconfigured — `88c04cf5-…` has 1 row in `driver_finance.driver_pay_rates`, the only USMCA rate of 93 on prod. **FIX REQUIREMENT — one decision, money lane owns it:** either repoint the office control to `/dispatch/loads/:id/transition`, or give `/mdata/loads/:id/status` the same two calls. Do NOT duplicate the latch logic in a third place. **GUARD:** assert that every code path which writes `mdata.loads.status` to a delivered/in-transit state also invokes the revrec latch + settlement ping — a guard over either endpoint alone reproduces this exact miss, because each endpoint is internally consistent. Mutation-prove. | — | **OPEN (P0) — CC-3-proven live 2026-08-06, awaiting CC-1** |
| **OPEN** `LV-TXN-005` — the two status endpoints accept DIFFERENT status vocabularies, and the Kanban renders a lane the API rejects | — | C | **CC-2 / mechanical+FE+route contract** | Found alongside LV-TXN-004. `PATCH /api/v1/mdata/loads/:id/status` accepted `dispatched`/`at_pickup`/`in_transit`/`at_delivery`/`delivered` (all 200) and **rejected `loaded` with 400 `validation_error`**. `PATCH /api/v1/dispatch/loads/:id/transition` **rejects `delivered` outright** — its enum is `unassigned\|assigned_not_dispatched\|dispatched\|in_transit\|delivered_pending_docs\|…` — and reports the SAME load as `delivered_pending_docs` where the mdata path calls it `delivered`. **The Kanban board renders a "Loaded" column** (observed live, between "At pickup" and "In transit"); dragging a card into it calls the mdata endpoint with `new_status:'loaded'` → **400**, i.e. the product draws a lane its own API refuses. **FIX REQUIREMENT:** one status vocabulary shared by both endpoints and the board, or the board must not render lanes the write path rejects. **GUARD:** assert the Kanban column status list is a subset of the accepted enum of whatever endpoint `onStatusDrop` calls. | — | **OPEN (major) — CC-3-proven live 2026-08-06, awaiting CC-2** |
| **OPEN (P0)** `LV-TXN-007` — `CLS-DISP-WIRE-07`'s documented root cause is WRONG; it is the SAME defect as LV-TXN-004 | — | C | **CC-1 (money)** — consolidate with LV-TXN-004 | **CONTROLLED A/B PROVEN LIVE by CC-3 on USMCA.** The wave-queue records WIRE-07 as *"reads `body.data.delivered_at` from the request — if the client doesn't send it, it's NULL"*. **That is factually wrong:** `stampFinalActiveDeliveryDeparture` (`dispatch/stamp-final-delivery-departure.ts:42`) writes `SET actual_departure_at = COALESCE($2::timestamptz, now())` — omitting `delivered_at` falls back to **`now()`** and can never write NULL. A builder told to "make the client send delivered_at" would change nothing. **THE EXPERIMENT — two loads, same entity, same customer, same driver `88c04cf5-…`, same unit `bb1e77ab-…`, same day, NEITHER call sending `delivered_at`; the ONLY variable is the endpoint:** `L-20260802-0258` advanced via `PATCH /mdata/loads/:id/status` (**what the Kanban calls**) → **0 of 2 stops stamped**; `LUSMCAFREIGHT-20260806-0001` (`678fc733-…`, created as the control) advanced via `PATCH /dispatch/loads/:id/transition` → **1 of 2 stamped ✓** (1 is CORRECT — the stamp targets only the final active delivery stop by design). **The stamp works; the office path never reaches it.** **CONSOLIDATION — this is ONE fix, not two classes:** WIRE-07 is registered as the *"CRITICAL-PATH ROOT BLOCKER"* for WIRE-05 (revrec) and WIRE-04 (delivery-evidence invoice gating); those, plus HOLD-001, plus this all trace to the single missing call in LV-TXN-004. **Fixing LV-TXN-004 should be expected to move FOUR open classes** — size the work accordingly and re-measure all four after. **HONEST LIMIT, do not over-claim:** on the control load `accounting.posting_batches` = **0**, so even the correct endpoint did NOT post revenue recognition. That is a SEPARATE unresolved question (flag gating, or revrec may require POD/invoice which the control load has neither) and must not be assumed fixed by the same change. **GUARD:** the WIRE-07 guard must assert the stamp happens for whatever endpoint the OFFICE UI actually calls — a guard pointed at `/transition` alone passes today while the product is broken, which is precisely how this survived. They are not.** **REPRODUCED LIVE on deployed `e6343f4`:** posted `POST /api/v1/accounting/bills` **twice with the SAME `Idempotency-Key`** → **both HTTP 201**, returning **two DIFFERENT bill ids** `304f5fa3-8064-4eca-957c-d48249573184` and `d613ca88-9f4b-4840-93a3-140847841453`. **Confirmed on Neon prod:** two rows in `accounting.bills`, identical `bill_number='CC3-BILL-0001'`, identical `amount_cents=12345`, identical `vendor_id`, both `status='unpaid'`, both `qbo_bill_id` NULL (TMS-native), created **295 ms apart** (19:11:43.818Z / 19:11:44.113Z). **That is DUPLICATED ACCOUNTS PAYABLE from a single retry.** **ROOT CAUSE — the store never runs.** `apps/backend/src/middleware/idempotency.ts` is written correctly on the read side: the preHandler looks up `prior`, returns **409** on a hash mismatch and replays the cached response on a match. But **`public.idempotency_keys` has `n_live_tup` 0, `n_tup_ins` **0**, `n_tup_del` 0 — the table has NEVER held a single row.** With nothing ever stored, `prior` is always null, so every replay is treated as a brand-new request. The `onSend` hook bails silently before storing — the prime suspect is its own guard `const userId = req.user?.uuid; if (!userId || !UUID_RE.test(userId)) return payload;` (it also skips non-2xx and Buffer payloads), and any throw inside `deps.store` is caught. **The enforcement half is live; the memory half has never once executed.** **SECOND LAYER ALSO ABSENT:** both rows share `bill_number='CC3-BILL-0001'`, so there is **no unique constraint** on bill number per vendor/entity either. Nothing at any layer prevents the duplicate. **WHY THIS IS THE MOST DANGEROUS SHAPE:** a control that 400s when absent is a control users and integrations TRUST. A double-click, a proxy retry, or a client retry loop silently creates a second payable — and duplicate payables become duplicate payments. It is latent today only because TMS-native bill volume is ~10 rows. **FIX REQUIREMENT:** make the store actually execute and fail LOUD if it cannot (a swallowed store failure is what made this invisible); assert the row lands in `public.idempotency_keys` in the same transaction as the write it protects. Add the `UNIQUE (operating_company_id, vendor_id, bill_number)` backstop separately. **GUARD:** replay the same Idempotency-Key against a money-write endpoint and assert the SECOND call returns the SAME resource id (or a replay header), plus assert `idempotency_keys` is non-empty after a protected write. **Mutation-prove by disabling the store and confirming RED** — a guard that only checks "400 when the header is missing" passes today while the protection does not exist, which is exactly how this survived. **CLASS NOTE — this is the THIRD vacuous control found this session**, after LV-TXN-009 (fuel-overage dedupe reading two FKs nothing writes) and LV-TXN-013 (WIRE-06 guard asserting against an empty CI database). Recommend a standing sweep: **every control whose protection depends on a table must assert that table is non-empty on the path it protects.** | — | **OPEN (P0) — CC-3-reproduced live 2026-08-06** |

Verified with `git check-attr merge`: of the four, **only CLAIMED-NUMBERS.json had a union rule**; the other three fell through to plain 3-way merge. **FIX (CC-1, this PR):** `docs/law/LAW.json` + `scripts/.guard-exempt.json` -> `merge=json-union`; `docs/audit/GUARD-WORKORDERS.md` -> git's built-in `merge=union` (markdown, so no JSON driver applies). LAW.json is a BARE ARRAY, and the generic array union dedupes by structural identity — two lanes editing the SAME law differently would have produced two rows of law claiming one id — so the driver gained a top-level id-keyed union that unions disjoint ids and REFUSES (leaves a real conflict) on a same-id disagreement, because two lanes disagreeing about what a law says is for a human, not a merge driver. **CC-2: take NO action for this and do NOT rebase for it** — the fix is upstream in `.gitattributes` and applies to every lane the moment it lands. The first read came off a rollup status field instead of the job record, which is the `expected-state-recorded-as-failure` anti-pattern. Those two are **UNVERIFIED — needs live check when the run lands**, not defects. The one remaining cause that IS confirmed theirs: `locked-guards` on #4612 (3 placeholder `acceptance[]` files). **PERMANENT RULE — resolve hot-file conflicts LOCALLY, never in the GitHub UI or via `gh pr update-branch`:** GitHub's server-side merge does not run the repo's custom `json-union` driver, so a conflict resolved there is resolved by hand, and hand-resolving an append-only registry means picking a side — which silently destroys another lane's rows. Locally, `npm install` registers the driver via the `prepare` script and `git merge origin/main` unions them with no conflict at all. | this PR | — |

THIS IS A CROSS-CONNECTION READ-YOUR-OWN-WRITES BUG.** Call path read end to end: `loads.routes.ts:1166` enters `withCompanyScope` -> `:357-368` -> `withCurrentUser` -> **`pool.connect()` + `BEGIN` (connection A)**; the handler stamps `mdata.load_stops.actual_departure_at` at **`:1295`** on **A, uncommitted**; then calls `postLoadRevenueLatch` at **`:1335`**, which enters **`withLuciaBypass` (`auth/db.ts:158-166`) -> `luciaPool.connect()` + its OWN `BEGIN` = **connection B**; the evidence gate `finalActiveDeliveryDepartureAt` (`poster.service.ts:277-298`) then runs **on B**. **B CANNOT SEE A'S UNCOMMITTED STAMP under READ COMMITTED** -> reads NULL -> gate `missing_delivery_evidence` -> no-op. A then commits, the departure becomes visible, and **nothing ever retries.** **THIS EXPLAINS EVERY FACT INCLUDING THE CONTRADICTORY ONES:** the departure IS present now (A committed); zero latch rows (poster no-opped before that commit); **all nine gates pass when queried post-hoc because I queried on a fresh connection AFTER the commit — the gates were never wrong, they were evaluated when the evidence did not yet exist**; and **NOTHING was logged anywhere, not even by the deliberate swallow-and-log — because a gate is a RETURN VALUE, not a throw, so the `catch` never fires. The swallow is NOT why this was invisible; it would have been invisible without it.** TRANSP `L-20260624-0083` posted both events precisely because its departure was captured by the DRIVER path in an EARLIER, ALREADY-COMMITTED request. **FALSIFIABLE PREDICTION THAT MATCHES THE DATA: the latch fails exactly when stamp and latch happen in the SAME request (the office path, ACCT-F81) and succeeds when the departure was committed by an earlier request (driver path).** ACCT-F81 added the office stamp to make office deliveries recognizable and, for the same-request case, achieved the opposite — its comment shows the timestamp dependency was understood but the CONNECTION BOUNDARY was not. **★ FIX — ONE OPTION DOES NOT WORK: do NOT pass the route's `client` into the poster.** The poster requires the RLS bypass context (`SET LOCAL app.bypass_rls='lucia'` + sentinel GUC) that `withLuciaBypass` establishes; connection A lacks it. **WORKS, in order: (1) fire the latch AFTER the transaction commits** (post-commit, outside `withCompanyScope`) — preserves the bypass, preserves the never-500-the-driver policy, makes the evidence unambiguously visible; **(2)** pass the departure timestamp into the poster as an input so gate 7 is satisfied from the caller's own knowledge (narrower, couples caller to gate). **★ GUARD — THE EXISTING ONE CANNOT EVER CATCH THIS.** `verify-delivery-evidence-latch-wired` passes today and always would have, because **the call IS wired — wiring was never the problem. A static wiring scan cannot catch a visibility bug.** The guard that catches it is a LIVE LEDGER assertion: a load whose highest-sequence active delivery stop has `actual_departure_at` set, status `delivered`/`delivered_pending_docs`, flag on, non-TRK, **must have a balanced `earn` row in `accounting.load_revenue_recognition_postings`** — scoped to TMS-native loads (origin test). **SELF-CORRECTION, STATED PLAINLY: item 39 concluded "never invoked" and that single inference was WRONG.** Every measurement in item 39 stands; it is *because* all nine gates passed post-hoc that timing was the only explanation left. **SEE ALSO the SEPARATE defect `LV-BULK-DELIVER-NOLATCH` (item 41) — do NOT merge the two fixes.** Full record: items 39, 40. | — | **OPEN (CRITICAL) — ROOT CAUSE PROVEN 2026-08-07** |

So bill-void advertises behaviour that currently cannot execute. **★★ ROOT CAUSE FOUND — ONE LINE: `apps/backend/src/accounting/bills.service.ts:1764` → `const originalDate = String(billRaw.bill_date).slice(0, 10);`** The `pg` driver returns a Postgres `date` column as a **JS `Date` object**, so `String(new Date('2026-08-06'))` = `"Thu Aug 06 2026 00:00:00 GMT…"` and `.slice(0,10)` = **`"Thu Aug 06"`** — passed as `originalDate` into `postVoidReversal`, producing the exact live error. The line assumes an ISO string. **★ THE SURROUNDING CODE RESOLVES THE WHOLE PUZZLE:** the comment above it reads *"Post the reversing JE BEFORE the status flip so both land atomically on this client"* and the block is `if (flagOn)` — gated on `MONEY_CONTROL_VOID_REVERSAL_ENABLED`, verified **true for USMCA**. So **(1) the reversal IS implemented — correcting the earlier characterisation that bill void "doesn't reverse"; it does, and the intent is correct; (2) it throws on the date before posting; (3) because the reversal runs BEFORE the status flip in one transaction, the throw rolls the ENTIRE void back — which is why the bill was left un-voided with no reversal and no partial state (correct transactional behaviour around a broken input); (4) it explains the 4 out-of-band voids — this path cannot complete, so those rows were set by direct SQL that bypassed this block.** **★★ CLASS BOUNDED + THE FIX ALREADY EXISTS IN THE CODEBASE.** `postVoidReversal` has **five** production callers; **exactly ONE is broken**: (1) **`bills.service.ts:1764`** `String(billRaw.bill_date).slice(0,10)` ❌ **BROKEN (proven live)**; (2) **`invoices.routes.ts:831-832`** ✅ **CORRECT — type-checked**: `typeof rawDate === "string" ? rawDate.slice(0,10) : new Date(rawDate).toISOString().slice(0,10)`; (3) `journal-entries.service.ts:555` ✅ passes `entry_date` raw (proven live, item 32); (4) `amortization-posting.service.ts:823` ✅ raw; (5) `loan-payment-posting.service.ts:395` ✅ raw. **THE FIX IS A COPY OF `invoices.routes.ts:831-832` INTO `bills.service.ts:1764` — the correct defensive implementation of this exact computation already exists three files away for the sibling document type. Worth checking first: `obligation-reconcile.routes.ts:242` (same `bill_date` column) and `invoice-send.service.ts:216` (invoice send path). **GUARD:** assert the void payload serialises dates as ISO; add a round-trip test that voids a bill and reads back `voided_at` + a reversal; consider banning `String(...).slice(0,10)` on `date`/`timestamp`-sourced values. **SELF-CORRECTION:** earlier rows described bill void as "does not reverse" — correct about the DATA, wrong about the MECHANISM. The duplicate was correctly identified and the bills correctly marked void — **but the overstatement it was meant to correct is still there.** **WHAT IS NOT WRONG (stated fairly):** originals are preserved — all 4 voided bills still exist with `voided_at` set, none deleted, so that half of WORM holds; siblings untouched (`5a1d7268`, `304f5fa3`, `0fe49cd0` all live). The defect is precisely and only the missing reversing entry. **★ THE REVERSAL FLAG WAS ON — 'it was gated off' is RULED OUT (prod `lib.feature_flag_overrides`):** `MONEY_CONTROL_VOID_REVERSAL_ENABLED` = **true/true/true** (TRANSP/TRK/**USMCA**); `VOID_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED` = true/true/true; `WO_VOID_ENABLED` = true/true/true; `VOID_QBO_MIRROR_ENABLED` = false/false/false (**correct — QBO write-back never on; do NOT change this flag in the fix**). **The control was ENABLED for USMCA and the reversal still did not post — this is a code defect in the void path, not configuration.** (Same key-default-vs-override pattern as `LV-BANKFLAG-STALE`: CI describes the key as 'per-entity, default OFF' while the per-entity override is ON; effective state is what matters.) **★ VERIFIED AT JOURNAL-ENTRY LEVEL TOO (2026-08-07) — the ledger never learns the bill was voided.** `accounting.journal_entries` carries its own reversal columns (`reversed_by_je_id`, `reverses_je_id`, `voided_at`), so the finding was re-checked there rather than resting on posting-level columns alone. **USMCA JE census: 19 total, ALL `status = posted`, 0 voided, 0 with `reversed_by_je_id`, 0 that are a reversal.** The three JEs behind the voided bills are each **still `status = posted`, not voided, no reversal**: `811cb8bc-99dd-41ab-8822-1a9219d94a6c` (CC3-BILL-20260806-01, $743.21), `570ba3b6-7795-4cd7-98c5-596af46a501e` ($450.00), `faeccb0d-cbc3-4fc0-b2b8-9be7206cf7b5` ($450.00) — summing to **$1,643.21**, matching the residue from an independent direction. **SHARPEST STATEMENT: the bill is voided, but its journal entry is still `posted`. Void currently touches only the subledger document; the GL is left untouched.** Those are three exact JE targets for the back-post, and the fix must address the journal entry, not merely stamp `voided_at` on the bill. **★★ REFINEMENT 2026-08-07 — THERE IS A WORKING REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION; ALSO CORRECTS MY OWN WORDING.** JE `43b3ed80-c2f6-4fa1-a53d-6ba0d480697c`, memo **"Void reversal of invoice e4d2ebdd-…"** dated **2026-08-02**, is `posted`/`auto` with legs **`1100 credit $1.00` | `4000 debit $1.00`** — a correct flip of an invoice posting. **So (1) the reversal capability EXISTS and has fired correctly for INVOICE void — this is NOT a missing framework and NOT "reversal never worked"; BILL void simply does not do what INVOICE void does. That is a separate audit-trail weakness: any report or guard netting originals against reversals cannot do it by join. **REVISED FIX: (a) bill void must emit the reversing JE that invoice void emits — copy that path; (b) populate `reverses_je_id`/`reversed_by_je_id` whenever a reversal is emitted.** **SELF-CORRECTION:** my earlier wording "no reversing journal entry exists anywhere on this entity" was true of the `reverses_je_id` CENSUS but overstated as prose — a reversal JE does exist, it is simply unlinked, which is why the column-based census read zero. The measurement was right; the sentence around it was too broad. **★★ DISCRIMINATOR 2026-08-07 — THREE VOID IMPLEMENTATIONS, THREE QUALITY LEVELS. The reversal service EXISTS and is CORRECT; bill void never calls it.** Voided JE `ff286e60` through the UI (drawer promises *"an equal-and-opposite reversing entry"*) and it delivered perfectly: reversal JE `ea6a0f05-39df-465d-ba69-1385ccdf57ab` with legs **6160 credit $1.00 | 1000 debit $1.00** — the exact opposite of the original **6160 debit | 1000 credit** — original preserved as `posted`, `void_reason` captured, and **BOTH linkage columns populated** (`ff286e60.reversed_by_je_id = ea6a0f05`, `ea6a0f05.reverses_je_id = ff286e60`). **THE LADDER:** **JE void** = reversal ✅ + both links ✅ (`ff286e60`↔`ea6a0f05`); **invoice void** = reversal ✅ + links ❌ NULL (`43b3ed80`); **bill void** = **no reversal at all** ❌ (`811cb8bc`/`570ba3b6`/`faeccb0d`). **THE FIX IS NOT TO WRITE REVERSAL LOGIC — it is to make bill void do what JE void already does.** JE void is the best-in-class reference in this codebase. **RECOMMENDED ORDER: (1) point bill void at the JE-void reversal service — clears the $1,643.21; (2) add linkage population to invoice void so its reversals are traceable; (3) back-post reversals for the 3 already-voided bills.** **SELF-CORRECTION:** an earlier note here said `reverses_je_id` is "never populated" — WRONG; JE void populates it correctly, only invoice void leaves it NULL. **FIX REQUIREMENT:** the void path must post a reversing JE — DR 2000 / CR 5400 for each voided bill's original amount, same entity, linked via `reversal_of_line_id`/`reversed_by_line_id`, **reusing the existing poster (write no new GL math)**; then back-post reversals for the 3 already-voided bills so the $1,643.21 clears. **GUARD:** assert no bill with `voided_at IS NOT NULL` has un-reversed postings — for every voided source transaction `SUM(debit) - SUM(credit) = 0` across original + reversal, and every original posting has a non-null `reversed_by_line_id`. Shrink-only ratchet; it would have caught this the moment the void ran. **★ EXTENDS TO AR — IT IS THE VOID PATH, NOT A BILL BUG:** `INV-2026-00001` (void, $1.00, `voided_at 2026-08-05 23:16:48`) also carries **2 postings and 0 reversals**. It was voided TWO DAYS BEFORE the bill voids, so this is not a one-off from the recent remediation — it is how void has been behaving. (`INV-2026-00002`/`00004` are clean only because they never posted; `INV-2026-00005` correctly has 0 postings as a proforma.) **Fix at the VOID PATH, not per document type**, or the next voided invoice reintroduces it. **WORM CHECKLIST — 3 of 5 PASS:** (a) reversing JE **FAIL**; (b) original preserved **PASS** (all 4 bills + 3 invoices still exist); (c) append-only audit row **PASS** — 4 `audit.row_changes` rows on `bills` at the exact void timestamp, one per voided bill; (d) siblings untouched **PASS**; (e) entity balance **FAIL** ($1,643.21 residue). Both failures are the same single missing behaviour; everything else about void is correct. **Recorded by the live-verifier lane, which does not build.** Full record: `docs/audit/LIVE-TXN-BATTERY-2026-08-06.md` items 28 + 28a. | — | **OPEN (CRITICAL) — live-proven 2026-08-07** |

| **★ RE-SCOPE + BLOCKER** `LV-GLDARK-BLOCKED` — **the `CLS-SUBLEDGER-GL-DARK` P0 cannot be tested because `LV-STOPS-NOSAVE` blocks delivery** | — | C | **read before building the GL-DARK P0 — CC-1** | **LIVE-PROVEN 2026-08-06 (CC-3 live-verifier lane) on deploy `e6343f4`, USMCA.** Dispatching a load **auto-created its invoice in the same transaction**: `INV-2026-00005` (`d921fbde-b6e2-4e65-9e21-8bcf4278a862`) created at `17:02:41.402Z`, the exact load-creation timestamp. `total_cents 245000` = **$2,450.00** matching the load's `rate_total_cents`; `qbo_invoice_id IS NULL` (TMS-native); **`source_load_id` → the load and `customer_id` → the customer, both correct** (§10 reverse linkage PASS); line maps to income account **4000 Freight / Line-haul Income**. **So the load → invoice hop WORKS.** **BUT the invoice is `status = proforma` with `transaction_source_links = 0`, and that zero is EXPECTED STATE, not the GL-DARK defect** — under ASC 606 revenue is recognised when the performance obligation is satisfied, and this load has not been delivered. A proforma correctly does not post. **The blocker:** the only advance control is `Send`, which is **`disabled: true`** (verified in the DOM, not inferred from styling); clicking it changed nothing (`status` still `proforma`, `updated_at` still `17:02:41.402Z`, `gl_links` still 0). Proforma → final is almost certainly gated on **delivery / POD**, which is correct design. **And the load CANNOT be delivered, because `LV-STOPS-NOSAVE` means it has no delivery address.** **★ CONSEQUENCE: `LV-STOPS-NOSAVE` is on the critical path of the highest-dollar open card on this board.** Fixing stops is what unblocks proving (or clearing) `CLS-SUBLEDGER-GL-DARK` for the TMS-native AR path — prioritise it on that basis, not as a dispatch annoyance. **VERDICT RECORDED HONESTLY: `CLS-SUBLEDGER-GL-DARK` remains UNVERIFIED for TMS-native AR.** The live-verifier lane could not reach a finalized invoice and therefore neither confirms nor clears it — no guess is offered. Re-test once stops are fixed and a load can be delivered. Note this is consistent with the existing GUARD correction that QBO-origin invoices are correctly unposted; what is still untested is the **forward-only, TMS-native** path. | **OPEN** `LV-LOAD-EDIT-BLANK` — **the Edit-load form opens COMPLETELY UNHYDRATED on a live $2,450 assigned load** | — | D | **CC-3 / mechanical+FE** | **LIVE-PROVEN 2026-08-06 (CC-3 live-verifier lane) on deploy `e6343f4`, USMCA, load `L-20260806-0005` (`a0b1df25-…`).** Clicking **Edit** opens `Edit load` with **every field empty**: Trip type unselected (prod `NB`), Customer `Select customer…` (prod `TEST-Customer-One-20260806`), **Linehaul $0.00 and Total customer invoice $0.00** (prod **$2,450.00**), Truck unit blank (prod `TEST-UNIT-20260806-01`), Driver `Select driver` (prod `Juan USMCA-Battery`). The form's own banner says *"Editing persisted load details. Only fields you change are saved (partial PATCH — untouched columns stay)."* **WHY THIS IS DANGEROUS, NOT COSMETIC:** the partial-PATCH contract is the ONLY thing preventing this screen from destroying the load. A user who opens Edit, sees `$0.00` / `Select customer…` and re-enters what looks missing will overwrite correct data; and any control that reports itself "changed" on submit (a select firing onChange on re-pick of the same value, a currency input normalising `""`→`0`) silently zeroes a real charge. A dispatched, invoiced load is presented as an empty draft. **SCOPE HONESTLY STATED:** the live-verifier lane **did NOT submit the form** — it was closed and prod re-verified clean (`updated_at` still `2026-08-06T17:02:46.215Z`, `rate_total_cents` still `245000`, driver/unit/customer/trip_type intact). The data-loss path above is **reasoned, not demonstrated**; the destructive confirmation was deliberately not run on live data. The unhydrated form itself IS fully demonstrated. **★ SAME ROOT AS `LV-LOAD-UNASSIGNED` — TRIAGE AS ONE BLOCK, NOT THREE.** The load read/hydration path does not resolve driver, unit, customer, trip type or charges into the detail or edit surfaces, though the detail API returns correct ids and prod holds correct values. Fixing hydration once resolves the detail "Unassigned", the blank Edit form, and plausibly `LV-STOPS-NOSAVE`. **FIX REQUIREMENT:** hydrate the edit form from the persisted load (resolve ids → names/values, charges → line items, trip_type) before render; until hydrated, the form must not be submittable. **STANDARD:** an edit form must never display a populated record as empty — that is a data-integrity hazard, not a display nit. **GUARD:** assert the edit form for a load with a customer, driver, unit, trip_type and non-zero linehaul renders all of them non-empty; asserting the form merely opens reproduces the miss. Must be `--selftest`ed and mutation-proven. **Recorded by the live-verifier lane, which does not build.** Full record: `docs/audit/LIVE-TXN-BATTERY-2026-08-06.md` item 13. | — | **OPEN (major, data-loss risk) — live-proven 2026-08-06, awaiting build** |

(4) **No save request issued** — the only traffic after the click was `notifications?limit=20`, `notifications/unread-count`, `feature-flags/check?key=LOAD_WIZARD_V5`; no PUT/PATCH/POST. The button is inert. **This also EXONERATES the obvious first hypothesis** — that the create wizard's reservation-timer re-render (which regenerated the load number `0001→0002→0003→0005` mid-entry) had discarded the typed stop data. | **OPEN** `LV-LOAD-UNASSIGNED` — **load detail renders an ASSIGNED load as "Unassigned"; driver, unit and trip type all blank** | — | D | **CC-3 / mechanical+route** | **LIVE-PROVEN 2026-08-06 (CC-3 live-verifier lane) on deploy `e6343f4`, USMCA.** Load `L-20260806-0005` (`a0b1df25-e49e-4853-b8ee-a26b407e88ba`) is assigned on prod — `assigned_primary_driver_id 88c04cf5-…` (Juan USMCA-Battery), `assigned_unit_id 240be73b-…` (TEST-UNIT-20260806-01), `trip_type NB` — and the load-BOARD row renders all of it correctly. The load **DETAIL** panel, on the same screen, shows **DRIVER `Unassigned`**, **TRUCK UNIT `—`**, **TRIP TYPE `—`**. **ROOT CAUSE — missing projection in the detail query, NOT a FE bug and NOT an entity-scope bug.** `GET /api/v1/dispatch/loads/:id?operating_company_id=…` returns **200** carrying `assigned_unit_id` and `assigned_primary_driver_id` (both correct UUIDs, correctly scoped) but **no `assigned_primary_driver_name`, no unit-number field, and no `trip_type` key at all**. **The asymmetry proves it:** the same payload DOES resolve `customer_id → customer_name`, and DOES carry `assigned_secondary_driver_name` (null) — a name field exists for the SECONDARY driver but not the PRIMARY. `drivers: []` is also empty despite an assigned primary. The FE therefore holds only UUIDs for driver/unit and correctly falls back to "Unassigned"/"—". **WHY IT MATTERS:** a dispatcher opening an assigned load is told it is unassigned, inviting a double-assignment of the same driver and truck — precisely what the assignment board exists to prevent — and `trip_type` is hidden, which drives NB/TR/SB settlement closure. **FIX REQUIREMENT:** project `assigned_primary_driver_id → driver name`, `assigned_unit_id → unit_number`, and `trip_type` in the dispatch load-detail query, co-scoped to the load's own `operating_company_id` (per `CLS-JOIN-ENTITY-UNSCOPED` / `CLS-DISPLAYID-UNSCOPED`, resolve by id **AND** entity, never by bare id). Populate `drivers[]` or drop it. **STANDARD:** if the endpoint resolves a name for the customer and the secondary driver, it must resolve one for the primary driver and the unit — an assigned record must never render as unassigned. **GUARD:** assert the dispatch load-detail response for an assigned load contains a non-null primary-driver name AND unit number AND trip_type; a test that only asserts the ids are present reproduces this exact miss. Must be `--selftest`ed and mutation-proven. **Recorded by the live-verifier lane, which does not build.** Full record: `docs/audit/LIVE-TXN-BATTERY-2026-08-06.md` item 11. | — | **OPEN (major) — live-proven 2026-08-06, awaiting build** |
| **★ CARDS REFUTED** `LV-WF064-ALIVE` — **the WF064 / dispatch outbox lifecycle WORKS: producer AND consumer both fire** | — | C | **evidence for the two WF064 cards below — CC-1/CC-3 should STOP building against them** | **LIVE-PROVEN 2026-08-06 (CC-3 live-verifier lane) on deploy `e6343f4`, USMCA.** Two board cards state this lifecycle is dead: *"the whole WF064 lifecycle is inert end-to-end — wired, never executed"*, *"0 of 31,646 outbox events match `dispatch.*` / `%wf064%`"*, *"WF064-consumer dead (P1) … the settlement cycle never fires."* **All of that is a misreading of an empty queue.** I booked and dispatched ONE load through the real Book Load wizard (`L-20260806-0005`, `a0b1df25-e49e-4853-b8ee-a26b407e88ba`, USMCA) and **both events fired and were CONSUMED**: `dispatch.load.dispatched` created `17:02:41.402Z` → **`delivered_at 17:02:46.213Z`**, `retry_count 0`, `failed_at NULL`; `dispatch.wf064.override_notice` created `17:02:41.402Z` → **`delivered_at 17:03:10.184Z`**, `retry_count 1`, `failed_at NULL`. `dispatch.*` total **0 → 2**, `%wf064%` total **0 → 1**. **CORRECT CONCLUSION: the lifecycle was never EXERCISED, not broken** — no load had ever been dispatched in the TMS, so nothing had ever produced the event. The prior 0-count was accurate as a measurement and wrong as a verdict. This is the `expected-state-recorded-as-failure` anti-pattern applied to an event stream: an empty queue on a system that has never performed the triggering action is EXPECTED STATE. **ACTION: close the two WF064 cards as not-a-defect / never-exercised. Do NOT spend coder time "reconnecting" a consumer that demonstrably delivers.** Note the earlier GUARD correction was right that `n_tup_ins = 0` on `escrow_deductions_pending` is conclusive that no deduction ever landed — but that too follows from never having dispatched, and is not evidence of a broken consumer. Full record: `docs/audit/LIVE-TXN-BATTERY-2026-08-06.md` item 10b. | — | **EVIDENCE — refutes the 2 WF064 cards; no build required** |
| **OPEN** `LV-OUTBOX-ERRCOL` — **outbox writes SUCCESS detail into `last_error`, destroying the real failure message** | — | C | **CC-3 / mechanical** | **LIVE-PROVEN 2026-08-06 (CC-3 live-verifier lane) on deploy `e6343f4`.** Both successfully-delivered dispatch outbox rows carry a **success** string in the **`last_error`** column: `dispatch.load.dispatched` → `last_error = 'driver_instructions_distributed'`; `dispatch.wf064.override_notice` → `last_error = 'override_notice_delivered_to_3_owner(s)'`. Both have `delivered_at NOT NULL` and `failed_at NULL`, i.e. they are healthy. **Two consequences.** (1) Any triage query of the shape `WHERE last_error IS NOT NULL` returns **healthy** rows, so the column cannot be used to find failures — it silently inverts the meaning of the operator's most obvious query. (2) **Worse and concrete:** the override-notice row has **`retry_count = 1`**, so its first delivery attempt genuinely DID fail — and that real error text has been **overwritten by the later success string and is now unrecoverable**. The one row that could have told us why a WF064 notice failed has had its diagnosis destroyed by the success path. **FIX REQUIREMENT:** stop writing success detail to `last_error`. Put delivery outcome/detail in its own column (e.g. `last_result` / `delivery_note`) and leave `last_error` populated **only** on a failed attempt, never cleared or overwritten by a subsequent success — or append attempt history rather than overwriting, consistent with the append-only/WORM posture (§2). **STANDARD:** a failure-diagnosis column is write-on-failure-only; overwriting it on success is the observability equivalent of deleting the audit row. **GUARD:** assert no success path writes `last_error`, and that a retried-then-delivered row retains its original failure text. Must be `--selftest`ed and mutation-proven. **Recorded by the live-verifier lane, which does not build.** Full record: `docs/audit/LIVE-TXN-BATTERY-2026-08-06.md` item 10c. | — | **OPEN (minor/observability) — live-proven 2026-08-06, awaiting build** |
